# Proposed OEIS comments: A196901 to A203794

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

```

```

## A196906

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A196906>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8721](../papers/08501-09000/08721-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) + 3*a(n-3) + 2*a(n-4) - a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 + 4*x + 3*x^2 + 3*x^3 - x^4 - x^5) / (1 - x - 2*x^2 - 3*x^3 - 2*x^4 + x^6). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 01 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A197175>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [7180](../papers/07001-07500/07180-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) +6*a(n-2) -2*a(n-3) -5*a(n-4) -20*a(n-5) -4*a(n-6) +24*a(n-7) -4*a(n-8) -16*a(n-9) -3*a(n-10) +47*a(n-11) -24*a(n-12) -5*a(n-13) +3*a(n-14)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 15 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 15. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A197230

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A197230>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [425](../papers/00001-00500/00425-DISPROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +3*a(n-2) +38*a(n-3) +40*a(n-4) +10*a(n-5) -150*a(n-6) -375*a(n-7) -175*a(n-8) +637*a(n-9) +2013*a(n-10) +1368*a(n-11) -1614*a(n-12) -4361*a(n-13) -4043*a(n-14) +946*a(n-15) +4156*a(n-16) +3551*a(n-17) +552*a(n-18) -881*a(n-19) -629*a(n-20) -1033*a(n-21) +508*a(n-22).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This empirical recurrence is false. The entry publishes 22 terms with offset 1, so the recurrence first says something at n = 23, and it already fails there: the true value is 1327965802062332 and the recurrence gives 1327965802062198. The sequence is a transfer-matrix count, hence C-finite, and its true minimal recurrence has order 25; its first 22 coefficients are exactly the ones above, so the line is the correct recurrence with its last three terms dropped. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A197403>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8031](../papers/08001-08500/08031-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) + 7*a(n-3) - 10*a(n-4) - 39*a(n-5) - 30*a(n-6) - 12*a(n-7) + 5*a(n-8) + 2*a(n-9).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + x)^2*(3 - 3*x - 7*x^2 - 9*x^3 - 3*x^4 + 8*x^5 + 2*x^6) / (1 - 2*x - 4*x^2 - 7*x^3 + 10*x^4 + 39*x^5 + 30*x^6 + 12*x^7 - 5*x^8 - 2*x^9). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 01 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A200753

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A202048

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A202048>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2461](../papers/02001-02500/02461-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/30)*n^6 + (9/10)*n^5 + (59/6)*n^4 + (111/2)*n^3 + (2552/15)*n^2 + (1278/5)*n + 144.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, May 25 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 157 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A202414

Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9482](../papers/09001-09500/09482-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +a(n-2) +3*a(n-3) +a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(34 + 26*x + 43*x^2 + 13*x^3) / (1 - 2*x - x^2 - 3*x^3 - x^4). - _Colin Barker_, May 28 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 7 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A202440

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A202440>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3191](../papers/03001-03500/03191-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (2/3)*n^3 + 8*n^2 + (100/3)*n + 34 for n>1.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, May 28 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 43 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2330](../papers/02001-02500/02330-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 7*a(n-1) -3*a(n-2) +19*a(n-3) -3*a(n-4) +25*a(n-5) +24*a(n-6) -22*a(n-7) -16*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(2 + 4*x + 5*x^2 + 20*x^4 + 7*x^5 - 16*x^6 - 8*x^7) / (1 - 7*x + 3*x^2 - 19*x^3 + 3*x^4 - 25*x^5 - 24*x^6 + 22*x^7 + 16*x^8). - _Colin Barker_, Jun 01 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 73 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A203175

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A203175>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3474](../papers/03001-03500/03474-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 2*a(n-3) = A003229(n-4)+A003229(n-2).
> Empirical G.f.: -x*(1+x^2) / ( -1+x+2*x^3 ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 02 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 12 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---
